# Resource List

> Categorised links for **Muse** (Meta's personal AI agent). Types are labelled; star counts are approximate and re-checked periodically — the dated snapshot table lives in [`data.md`](data.md).
> A background job re-checks link liveness periodically — when a fetch fails from this host it retries through a proxy rather than reporting a dead link.
>
> 中文版 → [resources.zh-CN.md](resources.zh-CN.md)

| [Muse Code](https://dev.meta.ai/products/muse-code) | Programming-workflow sibling product |
| [Muse Code docs - "Working with the agent"](https://dev.meta.ai/docs/muse-code/interactive) | The session reference for the Muse Code CLI: it answered **HTTP 200** directly from this host on 2026-10-14 (260,210 B; `<link rel="canonical">` points at itself, `<title>Working with the agent - Meta Model API</title>`) and names the verbs **`muse resume <fork-id>`**, **`muse export`**, **`muse trace inspect`** and **`muse exec --no-session-log`**, plus the two in-session commands **`/resume`** ("pick a retained session to resume (this workspace)") and **`/fork`** ("branch a new session from the current point") |
| [Muse Code cookbook - "Audit and resume agent sessions"](https://dev.meta.ai/docs/cookbook/audit-agent-sessions) . ["Deterministic replay in CI"](https://dev.meta.ai/docs/cookbook/deterministic-replay) | The two recipes that show **where Muse Code keeps a session on disk**: one append-only log per session at `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/muse/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/<session-id>/session.jsonl`, each line an envelope `{sequence, recorded_at, record_type, durability, payload_type, payload}`, with subagents and observers writing their own logs under `.../<session-id>/subagent/<child-id>/` and session envelopes carrying `payload.kind: "run"` holding the run events the model-context projection is built from. The audit recipe (~35 min; harness *Muse Code (the muse CLI)*, model `muse-spark`) walks `muse export` and `muse trace inspect`, the latter replaying recorded events through Muse Code's own projection code with **no model call, no tool, no network**; the replay recipe turns a golden fixture into a required CI check. Both answered **HTTP 200** directly from this host on **2026-10-14** (the audit page after five failed attempts) |
| [Permissions help page](https://www.meta.com/help/1385290430137537/) | Per-connection permission settings |
| [Link purchase protections](https://support.link.com/questions/what-s-covered-with-protections) | What is actually covered: lost/damaged items, price drops, return fees |
| [App Store](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/muse-from-meta/id6760173601) · [Google Play](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.facebook.aura) | Clients |
| [Muse Code release channel](https://api.meta.ai/muse-code/channels/muse-stable) | The public update channel behind Muse Code: it answered **HTTP 200** directly from this host on 2026-10-12 with version **1.4.6-R6000.1** (`state`: `public`), and the manifest it names lists **seven artifacts** including `x86_linux` and `aarch64_linux` — see §3 for the full list |

The written guide works offline; the playable-demo page at `https://tenex-labs.github.io/muse-personal-agent-start-kit/` answered 200 on 2026-10-08, while the `…-start-kit-safe` path its README links answered 404 that day. Zero stars and no licence file — a community guide, not a vetted tool |

## 3. Open-source ecosystem

### Closest open equivalents

- **[CopilotKit/openmuse](https://github.com/CopilotKit/openmuse)** ★5.2k · MIT · TypeScript
  A personal-agent app that **brings its own agent computer**: persistent Chromium plus an optional Linux workspace; its own server / task worker / browser worker; works with any agent harness; iOS, Android and web clients. Read `docs/VERIFICATION.md` (what has been proven) and `ROADMAP.md` (what has not: graphical desktop, automatic checkout).
- **[HKUDS/OpenHarness](https://github.com/HKUDS/OpenHarness)** ★16.0k · MIT · Python
  Open agent harness with a built-in personal agent ("Ohmo").
- **[HarnessMD/munder-difflin](https://github.com/HarnessMD/munder-difflin)** ★8.9k (8,892) · MIT · TypeScript
  A multi-agent harness that buys nothing new: it wraps the terminal CLIs you already pay for — Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Crush, GitHub Copilot CLI, Cursor and more — and puts them on one office floor, with a "clone" of you coordinating the roster while agents message, route and remember. Its own tagline, verbatim: *"Agent harness to run an office of your clones"*. Electron · React · Pixi.js · xterm.js · node-pty; **1,416 commits · 60 contributors · 1,231 forks · 20 topics · homepage `munderdiffl.in` · latest published release v0.5.5 (2026-10-01T21:00:56Z)**, which the README's version badge already overshoots at 0.5.6. Created 2026-05-31, last push 2026-10-19T04:45:27Z. It competes with Muse rather than connecting to it (no Muse integration) and is not affiliated with Meta. Two lookalike names to ignore: `AgentMrX/munder-difflin` is a single squashed re-import (one commit, *"Import HarnessMD/munder-difflin (squashed) as Mr. Rao AI edition"*, ★0, created 2026-10-19T05:05:22Z, homepage pointing at `mrraoai.com`), and the downloads badge still points at the pre-move path `chaitanyagiri/munder-difflin`.

### Tools built around Muse itself

| Project | ★ | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| [czg86389-hub/muse2api](https://github.com/czg86389-hub/muse2api) | 382 | **Unofficial** bridge that turns the muse.ai web app into an OpenAI-compatible API — chat (`/v1/chat/completions` **and** `/v1/responses`), images (`/v1/images/generations` · `/edits`) and video (`/v1/videos`: 5 / 6 / 8 / 10 s, 9:16 or 16:9, first-frame image-to-video) — by driving a headless Chromium over the DevTools protocol with a pooled set of accounts, automatic 48-hour session renewal and a companion Chrome extension that exports accounts; FastAPI · Docker · **MIT** · Python, created 2026-09-25, last push 2026-10-11, **35 commits · 5 contributors · 108 forks · no release ever published**. It is the instance `d0nkey8urger-bot/magpie-muse2api-plugin` below plugs into Codex / OpenCode / magpie; it is not affiliated with Meta, it spends your own Muse account's web quota, and the project's own terms-of-service and account-risk caveats apply. (Endpoint list read from the project's README this round; the service itself was not called from this host.) |
| [avirtual/clodex](https://github.com/avirtual/clodex) | 129 | Manage fleets of Claude Code / Codex / Muse agents |
| [Wode44398/muse-bridge](https://github.com/Wode44398/muse-bridge) | 18 | One message deploys Claude Code onto your Muse VM; the repo now also ships *dimensio*, a multi-model agent workspace |
| [bex-co/muse-code-acp](https://github.com/bex-co/muse-code-acp) | 13 | **Unofficial** ACP adapter — drive Muse Code from Zed, VS Code and other Agent Client Protocol editors (npm `@bex-co/muse-code-acp`) |
| [BrokkAi/muse-acp](https://github.com/BrokkAi/muse-acp) | 14 | Dependency-free Rust adapter (Apache-2.0) that puts Muse Code inside Zed, IntelliJ IDEA and other JetBrains IDEs on your existing subscription, speaking the native **Muse Session Protocol** (MSP) over Agent Client Protocol; released on a rolling `v0.10.0`, with an installer for Zed settings |
| [EtienneLescot/muse-desktop](https://github.com/EtienneLescot/muse-desktop) | 4 | **The first desktop client for Muse Code in this list** — its README opens *"The desktop app Muse Code was missing"* and calls itself the harness that goes with the CLI: *"the kind of client Claude Code and Codex users take for granted, built for Muse"*. What it adds over the terminal: projects (a project is a folder; conversations group under the folder they run in, reorder by drag, and each carries its own model, reasoning effort and permissions, surviving a restart), git worktrees, sub-agent lanes, MCP servers with the tool catalogue **verified by a real handshake before it is saved** and remote tokens in the system keychain, a browser with tabs, a terminal that opens in the conversation's folder, a file tree, a diff review panel, a context meter with one-click compaction, and **opt-in computer use** in three levels — *Off* (the default), *Observe only* (screenshots and window reads, no clicks, no typing) and *Observe and act* (mouse, keyboard, clipboard) — granted as a list of named tools rather than a blanket allow, revocable at any moment, running on the open-source [cua driver](https://github.com/trycua/cua) (MIT) installed separately; approvals are bound to the conversation that asked for them and a stale one is refused rather than silently applied. Tauri · React · Vite, **MIT**, GitHub's primary language **TypeScript** — `/languages` this round: TypeScript 2,154,021 B · JavaScript 2,053,888 B · Rust 909,149 B · CSS 230,773 B · PowerShell 12,566 B · HTML 9,309 B · Shell 7,447 B · Python 1,830 B — created **2026-09-07T08:51:09Z**, last push **2026-10-12T18:30:59Z** (the merge of PR #238), 0 forks, 0 watchers, 1 open issue, 5 topics (`ai-agent` · `computer-use` · `desktop-app` · `muse-code` · `tauri`), not a fork, 17,907 KB, **843 paths** in the recursive tree, **1,133 commits** and **239 pull requests** on `main` — **all from a single contributor**, the owner, per `/contributors` (1,133 contributions). Releases: **four published, the latest stable being `v0.1.0` (2026-09-24T15:26:53Z)** with `Muse-Desktop_0.1.0_aarch64.dmg` (signed and notarised, so no Gatekeeper warning) and `Muse-Desktop_0.1.0_x64-setup.exe` (unsigned, per-user, no administrator rights); the README and `package.json` both say **0.2.0** and a `v0.2.0` git tag exists — *"Muse-Desktop 0.2.0 — browser driving (attach consent, acceptance proved), self-hosted sharing, SSH transport"*, tagged 2026-09-30T23:46:08Z — **with no GitHub release behind it**, so `/releases/latest` still resolves to 0.1.0. Platform reality in its own words: Windows x64 is the platform with a native qualification campaign behind it and needs **WSL with Muse already set up**; macOS (Apple Silicon) is the newer port; **Linux is not started**; and authentication plus model choice come from the Muse engine, not from the app. The README points at `docs/ROADMAP.md` as honest about what is measured and what is not, *"including the defects still open"*, and notes that some planning documents are in French. **Nothing was downloaded, installed or run from this host this round** — every behaviour above is the project's own description, read against its README, recursive tree, releases, tags, contributors and languages endpoints. |
| [theglitcharchitect/muse-skills](https://github.com/theglitcharchitect/muse-skills) | 3 | 23 Muse agent skills + a decision layer |
| [wlsdks/muse-agent](https://github.com/wlsdks/muse-agent) | 6 | Personal agent that keeps context across sessions |
| [jkxiongxin/muse-agent](https://github.com/jkxiongxin/muse-agent) | 2 | Chinese knowledge-base assistant |

| [harris-ryder/dowser](https://github.com/harris-ryder/dowser) | 0 | Read-only MCP connector built for the [Muse Connector Platform](https://muse.ai/platform): six tools (class-action settlements, CPSC recalls, unused card credits, expiring points, birthday freebies, "what's new"), each also a REST endpoint; MIT · TypeScript · created 2026-09-19, last push 2026-10-09 |
| [1clawAI/muse-connector](https://github.com/1clawAI/muse-connector) | 1 | **A live Muse Custom Connector run by a company rather than a personal experiment** — a connector for [Meta Muse's Custom Connector platform](https://muse.ai/platform) that moves an agent fleet's approval queue, wallet balances and activity into Muse ("is anything waiting on me?", "approve that", "what did my agents do today?"), with secrets, funds and policy staying in the 1Claw dashboard. Seven read routes plus **exactly one write** (`POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decide`); Muse holds an HMAC-signed `mcn_` connector token while the `plt_` platform key never leaves the service, which is stateless — revocation is disconnecting the app in 1Claw, and every request re-checks the connection. **Verified live from this host on 2026-10-13**: `https://muse.1claw.co/openapi.json` answered **HTTP 200** (5,737 B) and `https://muse.1claw.co/llms.txt` answered **HTTP 200** (1,618 B), the latter listing the routes and the assistant rules for reading an approval back before deciding it. Apache-2.0 · TypeScript · created 2026-09-19, last push 2026-10-13 — **9 commits, one contributor**, and `connector/SUBMISSION.md` is the submission kit written for Meta's functional / security / legal review, so **whether Meta has accepted it is not confirmed**. From the organisation [1Claw](https://github.com/1clawAI) (*"Secure Infrastructure for AI Agents"*, blog `1claw.co`, created 2026-02-19, 43 public repositories). |
| [xhluca/session-migrate](https://github.com/xhluca/session-migrate) | 138 | Vendor-neutral **session migration across 18 coding-agent harnesses** — Muse Code among them (support added in v0.8.0, 2026-08-25) — via `smigrate transfer` / `smigrate catalog search`; PyPI `session-migrate`, Python 3.11+ on Linux, 324 ordered routes, MIT |
| [nevoflux-browser/nevoflux-muse](https://github.com/nevoflux-browser/nevoflux-muse) | 0 | The client an AI agent runs in its own VM to drive a **NevoFlux** browser it has been paired with — MCP over an E2E-encrypted relay, with a resident bridge and the client protocol documented in the head's repository (`nevoflux-agent`, `docs/muse-client-protocol.md`). The `nf` CLI is non-interactive and takes `--json` throughout — `pair`, `auth begin`, `status`, `tools`, `call`, `approve` / `deny` / `pending` (an approval-gated call exits 3 with `approval_required`), `ensure`, `daemon`, `setup`, `unpair`, `reset`, `uninstall`, `version` — installed by `setup.sh` with a systemd-user unit or a five-minute watchdog (`ensure.sh`); the Muse side is pinned by sealed-envelope fixtures (`envelope.json`, `kdf.json`, `seal.json`, `pairing_code.json`, `mcp_messages.json`, `tool_errors.json`) behind a real `fake_head` / `fake_relay` harness with 18 test modules and CI, and a 24 KB agent-facing `INSTALL.md`, MIT · Python · created 2026-10-01, last push 2026-10-11 |

| [barkleesanders/muse-starter](https://github.com/barkleesanders/muse-starter) | 0 | A **starter pack for a Muse workspace**: `install.sh` copies `skills/` → `~/workspace/skills/` (auto-discovered by Muse's skill search), `agents/` → `~/workspace/agents/`, `commands/` → `~/workspace/commands/`, plus `MUSE.md` and starter identity templates (`SOUL.md`, `USER.md`, `MEMORY.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `TOOLS.md`, `IDENTITY.md`) — **never overwriting existing files**, with `MUSE_WORKSPACE` to put the workspace parts elsewhere. The tree backs the README rather than the description: 118 skill directories, 36 agent briefs and 22 command templates versus the 84 / 35 / 21 the repository description still claims; the pack is ported from a Claude Code starter, and its install block still clones `barkleesanders/claude-code-starter` (that repository exists). MIT · Shell · created 2026-10-01, last push 2026-10-12 |
| [odiumuniverse/herdr-agent-usage](https://github.com/odiumuniverse/herdr-agent-usage) | 0 | **Muse Code quota, context and cache meters inside Herdr** — the first quota-observability entry in this list. A Herdr plugin (Rust · MIT · one topic `herdr-plugin` · homepage `herdr.dev/plugins/`) that renders per-agent gauges for **Claude, Codex, Grok, Agy, OpenCode, Pi, OMP, Devin, Muse, Cursor and Kilo**; the Muse line is **Muse Code** specifically — its own README table gives the source as the *"CLI subscription endpoint; 5h and 7d"* windows, read from the *"current CLI account login; session via Muse's session lock (Linux)"* — plus a `~/.config/muse` config path, a macOS keychain login mode (`--provider muse`), `MUSE_AUTH_PATH`, and a documented failure mode: *"Muse quota is missing → run `muse login` (API-key logins have no subscription quota) … `herdr-agent-usage refresh --provider muse --keychain-approve` and click **Always Allow**"*. Muse is drawn with the text mark **◈** on purpose, because the bundled icon font (HerdrAgentIconsMax, OFL-licensed) has no Muse glyph. Created **2026-09-21T13:36:14Z**, last push **2026-10-12T12:50:47Z**, **294 commits**, **20 contributors**, ★0, 0 forks, 0 open issues, **no release ever published**. **The lineage is the caveat**: this is an *imported mirror*, not a GitHub fork — the API reports `fork: false`, and the head commit is `b505acf` *"Merge upstream levi-qiao/main a8408399 via dtbehnke mirror"*. The upstream it mirrors, `levi-qiao/herdr-agent-usage`, now answers **HTTP 404**, and so does the account **`levi-qiao`** itself, so the Muse support survives only in forks: [`dtbehnke/herdr-agent-usage`](https://github.com/dtbehnke/herdr-agent-usage) (a real fork, ★0 · MIT · created 2026-10-06, last push 2026-10-07) and this mirror. The best-known repository of that name — [`senna-lang/herdr-agent-usage`](https://github.com/senna-lang/herdr-agent-usage) (★48 · MIT · Go · created 2026-07-16, last push 2026-10-11 · 12 contributors · latest release **v0.5.20**) — is **not** the Muse one: a case-insensitive search of its README for `muse` returns nothing and its topics carry no Muse, which is worth knowing because it is the repository a search for this name surfaces first. Its owner, `odiumuniverse` (user **Azamat**, joined 2023-08-28, 16 public repositories), otherwise publishes Herdr-ecosystem tools (`herdr-diff-viewer`, `herdr-smart-nav`, `beadle`). |
| [finik/shepherd](https://github.com/finik/shepherd) | 3 | **A phone app for the coding agents running in [Herdr](https://herdr.dev) — Meta's Muse Code among them.** Its own one-liner, verbatim: *"A phone app for your coding agents in Herdr: Claude Code, Pi, omp, Codex, OpenCode and muse, over SSH. Flutter, Android."* The Muse side is **native, not positioning**: a dedicated transcript adapter sits in the tree at `lib/transcript/muse.dart` beside the Codex one, and Muse sessions are read from their own file — the README's connection diagram gives `~/.local/share/muse/sessions/…/session.jsonl` — with the app *"find[ing] the newest one started in the pane's directory"* because, in its words, *"Herdr reports no session for Codex or muse"*. Muse-specific behaviour it documents: Muse *"writes a summary of its own"* thinking, shown where Claude Code writes none; a running **muse** agent can be moved to another model, another reasoning effort, or both, from the chat's header; a session's cost is priced from *"muse's own price list"* when the agent does not record one; and Muse appears in the start-an-agent picker beside Claude Code, Pi, omp, Codex and OpenCode. Architecture in its own words: one SSH connection to the host — Herdr's Unix socket forwarded over `direct-streamlocal@openssh.com`, spoken in newline-delimited JSON (`session.snapshot`, `events.subscribe`, `pane.send_input`) — transcripts streamed over that same channel, a web page an agent is serving carried the way `ssh -L` would carry it (*"Nothing on the host is exposed to the network"*), and **nothing installed on the host** (the transcript readers are Dart string constants in the APK, sent down as heredocs); the host needs `sshd`, Herdr 0.9+ and Python 3. The limits are its own too: no terminal and no TUI driving, *"iOS has never been built or run"*, and any agent beyond its six *"is read with the Claude-shaped parser"*. A second integration on the same host, already tabled above: the subscription bars read the [herdr-agent-usage](https://github.com/levi-qiao/herdr-agent-usage) plugin's saved readings first and [CodexBar](https://github.com/steipete/CodexBar) second, *"and neither is required"* — that upstream URL answered **HTTP 404** when re-checked from this host on 2026-10-14, consistent with the entry above. Metadata read the same day (GitHub REST API, from this host): ★**3**, **MIT**, **Dart/Flutter** (Dart 684,288 B · Python 32,487 B · Shell 5,083 B · TypeScript 864 B · Kotlin 124 B), 0 forks, 0 open issues, no homepage, not a fork, **182 entries** in the recursive tree on `main`, 7 topics with **no `muse`** (`android` · `claude-code` · `codex` · `coding-agents` · `flutter` · `herdr` · `ssh`), created **2026-09-22T07:27:02Z**, last push **2026-10-13T17:00:05Z**, **one contributor** (`finik`, 29 contributions by the contributors endpoint) and **one published release**, `v2.13.0`, published at that same 17:00:05Z. Android-only and written by its author for himself; **nothing was installed or run from this host** — every behaviour above is the project's own description, read against its README, tree, releases, languages and contributors endpoints. |
| [LucasSwitz/musecraft](https://github.com/LucasSwitz/musecraft) | 0 | **A real Minecraft client for a Muse agent — the first in-world, game-client entry in this list.** Its description, verbatim: *"Musecraft: lets a Muse agent play Minecraft with a real client (skill, Fabric mod, avatar, evals)"*, and the README is addressed to the agent rather than the human: *"This file is written for a Muse agent. A user pointed you at this repository and asked you to install Musecraft. They will not read it. You do the work, and you tell them the few things only they can do."* The Muse side is the platform's own machinery end to end, not an allusion: the skill installs into `~/workspace/skills` (Muse's auto-discovered skill directory) through `install_skill.py`, the game is played out of a dedicated **side chat** created with `chat.create` (`context_mode: "fresh"`, name `Minecraft`) and driven by `chat.send_message`, the player itself is an exec-run CLI (`musecraft play` · `gather` · `goto` · `attack` · `chat` · `standby` · `stop`, one command per decision), and the human is asked for exactly two things — Muse's own network approvals (*"Installing and playing makes Muse ask them to approve network access … choose Always allow"* for the Minecraft, Fabric and Prism downloads, Microsoft, Xbox and Minecraft sign-in, the Figura avatar service and the game server's address and port) and one Microsoft sign-in with a link and a code, where *"Credentials never pass through you"*. Its turn economics are event-driven: `musecraft standby` is kept running in the background whenever a turn ends, because *"Idle play costs no turns; you wake when the user chats or the game needs you"*, and its exit is the wake-up — output that *"starts with Minecraft needs you"* and lists what fired (the owner's chat, chat naming the player, danger, an action finishing, blocking or interruption, death, respawn, joining, reconnection, the owner joining or leaving, the client down). Stack, from the tree: a Fabric client mod (Java 21, Gradle 8.12) bundling **Baritone 1.11.3** unmodified under LGPL-3.0 (`mod/libs/`), a Node controller plus CLI (`skill/scripts/*.cjs`), a **licensed Minecraft: Java Edition 1.21.1** client driven headlessly through Prism on a virtual display (`client={"display":":95","headless":true}`), Figura avatars (`skill/avatars/Jolly`, sources under `avatar/`), four skill references (api, player-model, player-prompt, server), three subsystem docs (event-driven play, navigation, building), seven contract tests under `tests/` and a release workflow that runs the checks on every push and cuts a **draft** release on a `v<version>` tag. The limits are the project's own: start Muse with `--disable-sandbox` (the review CLIs cannot run inside Muse's sandbox), Muse's built-in `plan` skill still wins a plain *"plan X"* request unless you run `muse skills user-only plan --scope built-in`, Muse reads only the first of `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, read-only scouts keep the shell and can spawn a child agent, `review.backend host` **fails closed** because Muse reaches one model family natively, and Muse also loads the Codex install's skills from `~/.codex/skills` unless `"context": {"foreign_personal_skills": false}` is set. | [dancingteeth/agent-looper](https://github.com/dancingteeth/agent-looper) | 3 | **A fix-until-green outer loop that treats Muse Code as a first-class worker *and* judge runtime** — `runtime: muse` embeds [`@muse-code/sdk`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@muse-code/sdk) (Developer Preview **0.1.1**, an MSP client over `muse serve`): the harness spawns one loop-lived `muse serve` in a detached process group, SIGKILLs leftovers and starts a parent-death reaper, then drives a **fresh session per outer-loop iteration** — default worker model `muse-spark-1.3-contributor` — with no chat memory carried between implement turns and `approvalMode: "allowAll"` plus an `onApproval` handler so an unattended loop cannot stall on a prompt. Setting `reviewRuntime: "muse"` makes Muse the judge as well, defaulting to **`muse-spark-1.3`** (the judge stays Cursor until you ask for it); `agent-check muse` smoke-tests the PATH CLI and the SDK, `META_API_KEY` is optional once `muse login` has been done, `reasoningEffort` is honoured on Muse runs, and `AGENT_LOOP_MUSE_TIMEOUT_MS` closes `muse serve` after **45 minutes** by default. Muse is deliberately kept off the `minmax` cost preset because, in the project's words, *"Spark is not Flash-class cheap"*. The loop itself: a frozen goal, a shell command as the finish line, an optional judge plus secondary reviewer, escalation on repeated verifier stagnation, and workers for Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Cline, Pi and DSH beside Muse — installable as npm `@dancingteeth/agent-looper`, with `pnpm add -D @muse-code/sdk` as the Muse peer. Metadata read from this host (GitHub REST API, 2026-10-20): ★**3**, **MIT**, primary language **TypeScript**, API size 1,811 KB, created **2026-07-04T01:01:48Z**, last push **2026-10-19T21:50:04Z**, 0 forks, **242 commits** on `main`, **20 topics** none of which is `muse` (`claude-code`, `codex`, `opencode`, `cursor`, `deepseek-harness` …), `package.json` at **0.7.6** while the latest **published release is `v0.7.4`** (2026-10-19T07:42:37Z); owner `dancingteeth` (**Paul Zgordan**, user since **2013-10-28**, 8 public repositories, 9 followers). **Nothing was cloned, installed or run from this host** — every Muse behaviour above is the project's own description, read against its README, `docs/muse-runtime.md`, `package.json`, and the tree, commits and releases endpoints. |

Its README states it was tested on Muse Code 1.4.4 (also Claude Code 2.1.294, Codex 0.156.1, Bun 1.4.2). - [gwyntel/muse-honcho-backfill](https://github.com/gwyntel/muse-honcho-backfill) — ★0 · licence *NOASSERTION* (the README states the `LICENSE` file is the **Mutualist License v1.2**, `MutuaL-1.2` — a non-standard licence the API does not recognise) · Python, created 2026-10-06T20:56Z, last push 2026-10-10T12:17Z, 0 forks, not a fork, no topics. A **memory loader** that turns a Muse data export into long-term memory: it reads the archive from the Muse app's *Settings → Data Controls → "Download your agent data"* (one `.txt` transcript per conversation), parses each into messages, splits it into episodes (a ≥4-hour gap between messages starts a new one) and loads them oldest-first in batches of 100 into a self-hosted [Honcho](https://github.com/plastic-labs/honcho) server, each message carrying its original UTC `created_at` so history lands *backdated* instead of all "today" — one session per episode, named `muse-<slug>-<yyyymmdd>-<hhmm>` and tagged with the `muse-export` scope. Re-runs are safe: a state file hashes every loaded message, so weekly exports only append what is new. The README is candid about the seams — the export does **not** mark side chats (the manifest flags every turn `channels=["main"]`), so the episode boundary is a heuristic and a side chat opened mid-flow lands in the surrounding episode; `[[hatch_widget:…]]` placeholders are stripped; the roughly half of turns that carry no chat text load as `[no message text in export]` so the true turn order survives; and messages over Honcho's 25k-char cap are truncated with a metadata note. The export itself is never committed (the state file defaults to a git-ignored `data/`). Muse relevance is **native, not positioning** — the tool exists to move Muse's own export into Honcho. Zero stars, no topics, four days old; tracked here, not in the table above, until it has a track record.

A **phone remote for the `muse` CLI**: a stdlib-only Python daemon binds `127.0.0.1:8787` and serves a mobile-first page from which you start a session, send follow-ups, read live output, stop a run and approve or deny — no app install. Sessions are persistent and pinned to one repo: the first prompt spawns `muse exec --json --session-id <id> --workspace <repo> --trust-workspace --prompt-file <file>` (the prompt travels as a file, so it never touches a command line), every follow-up resumes the same id, and several repos run at once up to `max_sessions` (default 4). Muse relevance is **native, not positioning** — the repository exists to drive `muse` (default `muse_command` is `["muse"]`), `--trust-workspace` is passed so skills behave as in a local run with no context-narrowing flags, and the README states the v1 seam plainly: an approval request parks the session as `awaiting_approval` and the decision is delivered as an explicit continuation turn in the same session, *"a v1 simplification, not a wire-level approval token"*, while a failed resume becomes `error` rather than silently starting a fresh session. Three read-only views sit alongside — `Projects` (live port probe, plus a proxy of `127.0.0.1:<port>` to the phone behind a 30-minute sliding single-project cookie, with the daemon's own `/api/*` keeping precedence and always needing the master token), `Files` (list-only, files cannot be opened) and `Changes` (VS Code-style git status with staged/unstaged diffs, but no commit or stage action in v1) — and their limits are stated (no dev-server WebSockets/HMR). Remote access is deliberately not an open port: the daemon listens on localhost only and the README routes the phone through Tailscale `serve` or an outbound-only `cloudflared` tunnel, with a single-user token printed once and kept in `.remote-agent-token`. Tests are offline by design (`python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` against `tests/stub_muse.py`, no model calls; `REMOTE_AGENT_PROVIDER=echo` for a dry run), and the tree is `.gitignore`, `README.md`, `config.example.json`, `remote_agent/` (`server.py`, `runner.py`, `store.py`, `gitview.py`, `static/index.html`) and `tests/`. The tree is `SKILL.md` (agent instructions and safety rules), `references/protocol.md` + `architecture.md` + `troubleshooting.md`, and `templates/` (`relay.py` — a Python stdlib login/daemon/status relay, plus `wechat-bridge.service` and `ensure-service.sh`). That adapter polls `inbox/` on a modest interval (the tested Muse setup used **5 seconds**), keeps a claim set so the same inbox file is never processed twice, checks each message's `from_user_id` against `secure/binding.json` and moves non-matching files to `inbox/ignored/` without replying, drafts a plain-text reply (Markdown renders poorly in WeChat), writes it to `outbox/<original>.reply.json` and only then moves the inbound file to `inbox/done/`, and can optionally mirror a compact summary into Muse's main chat. Binding detail is Muse-flavoured: it prefers the QR-login identity returned as `ilink_user_id` (with `ilink_bot_id` as the bot id), and first-message binding exists only when `WECHAT_BRIDGE_AUTO_BIND_FIRST=1` is set explicitly — kept as compatibility with *"the original private Muse deployment"*. Protocol status is stated as observation rather than guarantee (notes tested 2026-10-11; the upstream repository is `Tencent/openclaw-weixin`, MIT), and the project declares it is not affiliated with Tencent, WeChat/Weixin, OpenClaw or Muse. ★0 and a day old, so it stays out of the table above until it has a track record.

Its central claim, re-checked from this host on **2026-10-12**: the channel URL answered **HTTP 200 directly** (no proxy) with `{"channel":"muse-stable","version":"1.4.6-R6000.1",…,"state":"public","urgency":"none"}`, and the manifest it names listed **seven artifacts** — `x86_macos` 375,752,688 B · `aarch64_macos` 355,369,136 B · **`x86_linux` (`muse-x86-linux`) 360,958,168 B** · **`aarch64_linux` 323,098,728 B** · `universal_macos_pkg` 278,908,807 B · `x86_windows` 463,719,160 B · `aarch64_windows` 425,084,664 B — each with its own sha256, plus a top-level `msp_schema_fingerprint` for the manifest as a whole. That is why the tracked `JamSuite/eval-coding-agent-harness` entry above can note that Muse Code installs on Linux at all: Meta's public channel carries Linux builds for both x86_64 and aarch64 even though the product page lists macOS and Windows. Stays out of the table above because it is hours old, ★0, comes from a personal account, and wraps Meta's binary rather than calling a Muse API; tracked here instead.

Its limitations are stated rather than buried: *"Muse only sends tool summaries"* — no raw tool names, arguments or stdout, so names are inferred and match OpenClaw's core tools; reports often land *after* the reply text; *"Muse runs the tools, not Pi"*, only the latest user message is sent and Pi's system prompt is not forwarded; muse2api serializes turns, so *"one browser, one turn at a time"* with seconds of overhead; model selection is account-wide on Muse and is restored after each turn; *"No image input, no usage/cost numbers"* because Muse exposes no token counts; and *"Fragile by nature."* The disclaimer names muse.ai, Meta, OpenClaw and Earendil/Pi as unaffiliated, and the credits run muse2api (MIT), OpenClaw's agent/workspace model, then Pi's extension and tool-rendering APIs. What it is *not* is a way to **build** with Muse — it consumes Muse's output after the fact, and those 13 blobs are one binary. The project is also unusually candid about its own limits, which is why it is worth trusting where it does claim something: its status table marks the Gemini source *"UNVERIFIED format: step blobs are protobuf with no schema available; field numbers … were inferred from sampling"*, and marks Muse **"partly verified"**, stating that its *"original design note named `turn.input.user` / `run.output.delta`, but those did not occur in the 81 sessions on disk when this parser was written"*. The stated security model: nothing listens publicly by default and *"a public tunnel is a fallback, never the design"*; Tailscale Serve is preferred; the session secret is generated fresh on every launch (~190 bits) and destroyed on close, so *"there is no stored credential to leak"*; ten failed auth attempts in ten minutes pause auth for five minutes and push an ntfy phone alert; and the inherited rule is *"the URL is obscurity, not a control — the secret and the transport are the controls"*. `docs/ROADMAP.md` (dated 2026-10-13, and written as spec-from-a-chat rather than a plan) is a **steal list** from browser-use, UFO² and OmniParser — element-index interaction instead of pixels, domain allowlists, UIA fusion with vision only for what UIA cannot see, GUI+API hybrid preferring native app APIs over clicking, speculative multi-action planning (*"their claim: ~51.5% fewer LLM calls"*, labelled as UFO²'s claim), set-of-marks overlays and per-session trajectory recording — and it names the author's own `muse-brain-gateway` (model `brain/muse-brain`) as where *"Christopher's Muse context stays the brain"* in the alternative to building v2 at all. The most directly Muse-shaped rebuild in this section: its description says it copies Muse's *"orchestrator + background execution agent + Goal"* architecture, and the README's own diagram backs the claim — a **dispatcher** (`app/server.mjs`) calls the model directly with **no tools**, replies in one or two chat sentences within seconds, writes a ledger entry itself, turns a standing request into a **goal**, or hands real work to a queue; a **goal scheduler** wakes every 30 s, dispatches a goal-check task per due goal, and speaks in chat only when something actually changed; a **background task queue** runs at most **2 jobs in parallel** and streams state over SSE to a Tasks tab; and the **execution agent** is a **Hermes gateway on :8650** (search, social accounts, page building, image search, vision, memory, ledger, skills) driving a sandbox headless Chromium on **:8660** with its own profile for Weibo / Bilibili / X / Instagram / Weverse. Multi-user is code rather than a promise: invite-only accounts (scrypt-salted passwords, sessions stored as hashes), **one `HERMES_HOME` and one gateway per account** so memories, ledgers and sites cannot mix, SSE scoped to the owner with 404 for anyone else's id, and `scripts/user.mjs adopt` to migrate a single-user install — beside `deploy/` (systemd units, Caddy, backups) and a self-hosted Langfuse compose file for trace analysis. `hermes-agent/` is a **pinned git submodule** the README calls unmodified upstream, and the 58 bundled Hermes skills ship disabled. **Why it sits here and not in the table above**: three days old, ★0, no licence file, **two commits from two different names** (`hdy2001`, then `dongyanghe212`), Chinese-only README, and the model it is built on is LongCat rather than Muse — so the architecture is borrowed, the identity is its own, and nothing about it has a track record yet. A **job-search agent shipped as a Muse workspace distribution rather than a Muse client**: per its README the backend, bundled web UI and **111 typed MCP tools** come from the canonical Buboole app, and this experimental distribution keeps the full app while testing a board-first agent workflow — with the honest caveat that *"it does not add Muse as an outbound AI provider."* Muse is the host, not the model: the README reports Preview 6 passing **real Muse and GitHub Linux tests** (full-feed search, identical HTTP/MCP role IDs across all 111 tools, automatic startup/reuse, stop-and-restart with notes retained) and lists what is still unverified — human UI mounting, full-VM restart persistence, sustained background operation; Preview 7 adds readers for losslessly compressed `.json.gz` job details and drops the arbitrary 500-page search limit, with Windows reader/build tests passing and a fresh Muse-host lifecycle **not yet observed**. Operationally it is `python3 jobhound.py doctor` / `install` / `status` / `tools`, profile data kept outside the clone at `~/.local/share/jobhound`, one locked identity-checked loopback service that stops after ten idle minutes, and an optional foreground `serve` on 127.0.0.1:4176; the two workflows ship as skills (`skills/find-recruiters/SKILL.md`, `skills/interview-prep/SKILL.md`) with `AGENTS.md` as the entry point, and the README warns a fresh Muse conversation may not load the repository automatically. ★0, unlicensed, and every behaviour above is the project's own testing claim — nothing was cloned, installed or run from this host.

### Excluded on purpose

A keyword match is not a recommendation. Repositories whose description/README are essentially an **invite or affiliate link**, that point at a binary download which contradicts their own description, or whose *name alone* matches the search while the README has no Muse content, are left out — they are promotions, unrelated projects or worse, not ecosystem projects.

Not listed here; the watchdog re-reports it as "new", so this note is the answer.

- [JaredLodwick/muse-commons](https://github.com/JaredLodwick/muse-commons) — ★0 · **no licence file** · JavaScript, created **2026-09-27T05:36:32Z**, last push **2026-10-13T02:07:31Z**, 2,731 KB, 0 forks, 0 watchers / 0 subscribers, 0 open issues, no topics, no homepage, not a fork, default branch `main` at `4e76d126558ecd62f9530024429b2714ad647445` (committed 2026-10-13T02:07:31Z), **91 commits** (`/commits?per_page=1` returns a `Link` whose last page is 91). **A live WebSocket lobby for personal AI Muses**: its description reads *"A social room for personal AI agents — see who's online, watch them talk."* — agents `hello` in over a socket and appear as avatars wandering a room, and when two agents exchange messages their avatars walk toward each other, face off and talk, speech bubbles and all. The server is the authority on positions while clients only declare presence and talk events, over a JSON wire protocol any foreign agent can speak: `hello` (name, `serves`, `avatar:{color,emoji,image}`, `kind:"agent"|"viewer"`, `room`, optional `manifest_url`), `heartbeat` (every ~15 s; missing for 45 s means the agent "walked out"), `talk`, `say`, `create_room` (`visibility` public/private, `entry` open/knock/invite, a validated `category` of interest/local/utility), `invite`, `knock`, `admit`, `reject` and `post` (a `want`/`offer`/`intro` intent board whose `human_approved` flag the server enforces) feeding a `#marketplace` room with want↔offer matchmaking that auto-creates private deal rooms. It is a real codebase rather than a sketch — `server/lobby.js` alone is **297,675 B**, beside `server/protocol-v1.js`, `profiles.js`, `passport.js`, `reputation.js`, `threads.js`, `digest.js`, `ask.js` and `metrics.js`; `bridge/watch.py` (13,563 B) watches a muse-protocol inbox queue and turns every new `introduce`/`message` into a talk event, with sealed payloads rendered as `[sealed message]` and never decrypted; `deploy/` holds a drop-in hosting kit (`host-setup.sh` plus `muse-commons`, `muse-commons-bots` and `muse-commons-bridge` systemd units and a health check); and `docs/` carries nine specifications including `THREAT_MODEL.md` (20,477 B), `IDENTITY.md` (15,104 B), `STORAGE.md` (15,246 B) and `FEDERATION.md` (7,244 B). Two federation paths are claimed: inbound, where a foreign Muse proves its identity by sending a `manifest_url` that the server fetches asynchronously (5 s timeout, 64 KB cap, SSRF-safe) and marks `verified` / `unverified` with a roster badge, and a human-moderated lobby directory at `/directory`. **The Muse link is native without being Meta's product**: the room exists to visualize [`luke-hurd/muse-protocol`](https://github.com/luke-hurd/muse-protocol) (★1 · **no licence** · created 2026-09-26T14:38:52Z) — *"An open protocol for personal AI Muses to talk to each other"*, async typed messages, optional NaCl-sealed payloads, a `musefp/2` VM fingerprint and discovery via `/.well-known/muse-protocol.json`, *"No registry, no central server"*, with the author's own Muse ("Agatha") publishing a live manifest — and the commons owner also **forked** that protocol (`JaredLodwick/muse-protocol`, `fork: true`, created 2026-09-27T19:51:51Z) and runs `JaredLodwick/muse-experiments` (*"A shared playground for personal AI agents who met in the Muse Commons"*, created 2026-09-28). Its engagement with Meta's own surfaces is documented rather than assumed: avatars stay decentralized because, in the README's words, *"There is no central Meta API for other agents' avatars, so this stays decentralized like the rest of the protocol"* — each Muse publishes a portrait and points at it through a proposed `avatar_url` field on its manifest; the lobby exposes five read-only endpoints (`/api/places`, `/api/ticker`, `/api/presence`, `/api/board`, `/api/directory`) with `/openapi.json` and `/llms.txt`, "no auth, no keys"; and `connectors/muse/` holds a prepared pack for Meta's Muse Connector Platform — description, icon, example prompts, a ToS draft and `HOOKUP.md` — a **Raw API, no auth** submission to `https://muse.ai/platform` that the README says is still *"in the review queue"* and needs the domain plus HTTPS first. A `skills/muse-commons/SKILL.md` lets a Muse join the lobby itself, which the README says was *"verified live: test muse joined, spoke, left"*. It stays **out of the table above**: ★0, no licence file, two and a half weeks old, the protocol it speaks is community-built rather than Meta's, the connector submission has not been accepted, and its own roadmap still leaves the two real federation tests unchecked — *"Agatha (or another foreign muse) actually joins via a verified hello"* and *"outbound: Apollo joins a lobby hosted elsewhere"*. Tracked here, not in the table above, until it has a track record.

Any model, Claude Code & Codex over ACP, its own computer in a container."*). **Read from the source, not only the README**: `src/msp.rs` starts the child with `Command::new(muse)` and `.env_remove("META_API_KEY")` (line 141), the comment beside it giving the reason — Muse prefers that key over the account login, and this adapter exists to use the subscription; the client-side counterpart is a terminal sign-in method, `muse-acp login` → `muse login`, shown to the user as **Log in with Meta**. The protocol map is the README's own table: `initialize` (requesting per-session MCP and **declining Muse's own question dialogs**), `session/new`→`session/start`, `session/load`→`session/resume` with inline history replayed as updates, `session/resume` without history, `session/prompt`→`turn/start` answered when `turn/completed` arrives, `session/cancel`→`turn/interrupt`, the extension `_session/steering`→`turn/steer`, `session/update`→`item/*`, `session/request_permission`→`approval/request` → `approval/decide` *"one stage at a time"*, session modes→Muse's approval modes (on request, ask first, deny unlisted, allow all), and model / effort config options→`model/list` · `session/setModel` · `session/setReasoningEffort`; a turn that fails, *"such as a 429 when the subscription window is used up"*, fails the prompt with Muse's own message. The limits are its own: MCP over SSE has no MSP transport so SSE servers are **skipped**, audio prompts are **dropped**, Muse's `userInput/request` dialogs are declined at initialize because *"ACP has nowhere to show them"*, `usage/read` windows have no ACP field, and Muse's goals, workflows, plugins, recaps and forks *"are not exposed. Use them from `muse` directly."* Its one statement about Meta's surface is the **author's**, not Meta documentation, and is not reproduced here as fact: *"The model-only Responses API takes an API key and cannot use the subscription."* Package line: `muse-acp` **0.1.0**, edition 2024, `rust-version` **1.88**, on `agent-client-protocol =2.0.0` (features `unstable_auth_methods`, `unstable_end_turn_token_usage`), installed with `cargo install --git …`; `tests/bridge.rs` drives the adapter end to end *"against a scripted Muse host whose wire shapes come from a live `muse serve`"* and `tests/live.rs` puts the built binary in front of `muse serve --provider echo`, which *"needs no account or network, and skips itself when `muse` is not installed."* **Why it is recorded here rather than in the table above: ★0, one commit, and hours old at this read** — the watchdog surfaced it in this same round. What earns it the line is the author: `1broseidon` (*George Dikeakos*, a user since **2016-07-27**, **47 public repositories**, 30 followers, blog `chain.sh`) publishes `ketch` (★708 · MIT · Go — *"Fast, stateless CLI for web search and scrape. Built for AI agents."*) and `cymbal` (★352 · MIT · C) beside `hotline`. Not cloned, installed or run — everything above was read through the GitHub API and the repository's own files.

A **self-hosted, email-driven agent runner** that the README summarises as *"GitHub self-hosted runner, but the control plane is email"*: the cloud-side assistant runs `cloud/send_job.py` to build an **HMAC-SHA-384-signed** job email, the agent on your machine polls IMAP, checks the sender, the timestamp window and a replay seen-set, then runs the job's script and mails the result back — installed as a systemd user unit, a launchd agent, a FreeBSD `cron @reboot` line or a Windows Scheduled Task, standard library only on Python 3.9+, with the test suite driving the real runner against fake IMAP/SMTP servers, and a threat model stated flatly at the top rather than buried (*"This tool hands full administrative control of the machine to whoever holds the shared secret"*, the Gmail app password sitting in `config.json` in plaintext **on purpose** because it doubles as the signing key). **Its Muse link is one line and only one, and that was measured rather than assumed**: the sole occurrence in the whole repository is `README.md` line 3 — *"**Works with Muse, Grok, ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Copilot, etc.**"* — a case-insensitive scan of all **18** blobs finding **no second occurrence** (the only `meta` match, in `PROTOCOL.md`, is the word *metadata*), with no `muse` CLI call, no Muse plugin manifest, no Model API base URL and no Muse-specific code path anywhere; the README's own later lines call the runner *"SI-agnostic"* and promise that *"anything that can send email as the runner's Gmail account and sign the body can drive it"*, so Muse is named as one compatible assistant among six rather than integrated. That is a compatibility claim, not a base it is built on — which is why this row is dated here rather than earned. Two further cautions are worth carrying: the README's *"Easiest: download a release"* section offers three prebuilt platform archives (`homing-pigeon-windows-amd64.zip`, `-linux-amd64.tar.gz`, `-macos-arm64.tar.gz`) that **do not exist yet** — 0 releases, 0 tags, and the head commit is the CI work to produce them — and the owner is a farm-shaped account: `ctf-gooo-003` (no name, no bio, no blog, account created **2026-09-02T17:18:15Z**, **1 public repository**, 1 follower), where a GitHub login search for `ctf-gooo` returns **exactly two** accounts — this one and its empty sibling `ctf-gooo-001` (created **2026-08-12T04:25:53Z**, **0 public repositories**, 1 follower). Never open github.com in a browser to check the bridge: rendering pages burns tokens for nothing"* — and about not asking the user for a token: the `gh auth login` device flow, a code typed at `github.com/device`, *"no token creation, no scopes to pick, and this login does not expire like personal tokens do."* `muse-watch/` is a Linux daemon written for the Muse side of that loop: `install.sh <owner/repo>` keeps a read-only mirror so a status check becomes *"one small file read"* instead of a `git pull`, `state.json` carries every task's queue, state, progress note and heartbeat plus a `health` envelope (`last_ok_at` / `consecutive_failures`) rewritten every cycle, `events.log` keeps the last 200 transitions, and `healthcheck.sh` with a systemd **user** timer (`backseat-watch-health@<slug>.timer`) exists because, in its own words, *"a dead watcher used to be indistinguishable from a quiet bridge"*. **Two artefacts here are stronger than documentation.** A commit authored as **`Muse`** — `1377284`, *"v0.9.14: bridge reliability — timeout as a state, activity deadlines, worktree checkpoints"* (2026-10-14T02:55:05Z) — is merged into `main` by PR #40 at 03:01:04Z. And `BACKSEAT_BUGS_2026-10-12.md` is a field record, *"Captured by Muse during the voxel-survival-game UE5 task series, at Darek's request"*, whose table lists eight tasks (2026-10-05 → 2026-10-12) where the bridge's `timeout` verdict disagreed with reality in five of them — work that had actually landed as game-repo commit `d0b0ac6` against work that was lost — and every bug in it is marked *"RESOLVED in v0.9.14"*. **The Muse link is native but unwitnessed by Meta, and that was measured rather than assumed**: a case-insensitive scan of all 48 tarball files matches `muse` **97** times across **16** files — code comments in `vscode-bridge/src/extension.ts` (15), `logic.ts` (3), `sidebar.ts` (1), `package.json` (2, whose extension description names Muse and whose keywords add `grok`), `MUSE.md` (5), `AGENTS.md` (4), `README.md` (7), `CHANGELOG.md` (19), `BACKSEAT_BUGS_2026-10-12.md` (7) and `muse-watch/` (README 7, `watch.py` 6, `install.sh` 3, `healthcheck.sh` 1, systemd units 3) — while `meta.com`, `api.meta.ai`, `muse.ai`, `dev.meta.ai`, "Model API", `llama`, `lux`, `muse-code` and `muse serve` all return **0**, with no `muse` CLI invocation, no plugin manifest and no Model API base URL anywhere. The project says as much itself: `MUSE.md` carries the portability note *"Not Muse? This brief is named for Muse — Backseat was designed and dogfooded with Muse driving — but the protocol is agent-agnostic. Any agent with shell + git access (Grok, Dots, Clawdbot, …) can follow these exact steps"*, `AGENTS.md` opens *"You don't have to be Muse to drive Backseat"*, and v0.9.13 renamed the writer in `protocol.md` from "Muse" to "the architect". So what is Muse-shaped here is the workflow and the naming — the loop is built around what Muse does (poll on a schedule, spend tokens per check, swallow a night's queue, report back in chat), not around any Meta surface. What it is: an always-on personal agent assembled entirely from **Vercel's stack** — `eve` for agent-as-a-directory with durable sessions, channels, schedules and memory, **AI SDK v7** for the model calls and the tool loop, the **Workflow SDK** for durable execution, **AI Gateway** for one-key routing across providers plus web search, `@ai-sdk/harness-claude-code` to hand hands-on jobs to **Claude Code on the user's Claude subscription**, and **Vercel Sandbox** for the isolated machines — reached over **Telegram**, where only the owner's user ID passes a filter that runs before any model call, with long-term memory shared across conversations and scheduled runs, a one-minute dispatcher for routines the agent creates itself, and a 30-minute heartbeat that costs **no model call** when its checklist is empty.   **Its Muse link is a reference hook in the shipped code, not a name — and it is the first entry in this list that runs inside Muse's own hook runtime.** `connector/numby-hook.sh` (728 B) sources **`$HATCH_HOOK_RUNTIME`** and dispatches through `numby-bridge.py dispatch --dir` into two branches that call Muse's **`wake`** and **`silent`** verbs: *"Numby work available: claim each item with worker-start before acting"* with the payload when work is queued, *"Numby needs attention: report only; do not replay work or bypass approval"* when something needs a human, and `silent 'no dispatchable messages' '{}'` otherwise. `docs/RUNTIMES.md` draws the boundary itself: the Muse row reads *"Existing `numby-hook.sh` for a runtime providing `HATCH_HOOK_RUNTIME`, `wake` and `silent`"*, with the verification note *"Existing reference integration. Connector claim/receipt tests pass. Requires the actual Muse hook registration and an owner-approved phone test; a listener alone is not success."* — and the Hermes adapter beside it (`connector/numby-hermes.py`, 4,265 B) is marked *"Not yet tested against an installed Hermes runtime or a handset."*

  What it is: **a phone gateway, not an agent.** The README opens *"Your existing agent, in your phone's messages"* and describes *"an open-source phone gateway that connects your verified phone to your AI agent through Photon's shared iMessage numbers, with provider-dependent SMS/RCS fallback"*, saying plainly *"Numby handles phone verification, message routing and the queue. It does not host an AI model."* The tree is an application rather than a scaffold: a JavaScript routing service (`src/core.js` 16,937 B, `server.js` 19,924 B, `provider.js`, `media.js`, `dashboard.js`, `backup.js`, `restore.js`), a Python connector (`numby-bridge.py` 16,701 B), ten test files (`test/http.test.js` 17,274 B, `core.test.js`, `dispatch_test.py`, `connector_test.py`, `hermes_adapter_test.py`, …), `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (7,649 B) beside `docs/RUNTIMES.md` (7,043 B), a 21,103 B `public/guide.txt` capability matrix, an agent-facing `skills/numby/SKILL.md` (9,793 B), systemd units under `deploy/` and a static site under `website/`.

  Its own limits are stated rather than left to be discovered, which is the reason to track it instead of tabling it: Photon **shared numbers** rather than a dedicated line per agent, paired direct conversations only (no groups, calls, arbitrary-recipient outreach, edits or unsends), a 10 MiB / 8-part media ceiling, and *"No new handset end-to-end result is claimed"* — a provider-confirmed send is not proof of handset receipt, and Photon's plan limits and costs apply independently of this repository's MIT licence. The Muse Agent Discord link it carries is described in the README as *"a community invitation, not an endorsement by these products"*. Nothing was cloned, installed or run from this host beyond reading the blobs. Tracked here, not in the table above, until it has a track record.

## 4. Building one yourself — a recommended path

1. Read **openmuse**'s architecture (server + worker + persistent Chromium) to see how an "agent computer" is actually built;
2. Compare with **OpenHarness** for how the harness layer is abstracted;
3. Decide **your approval boundary**: which actions must never happen without a human (payments, sending, new connections). Muse turned this into a product-level default — it is the part people underestimate;
4. Decide **where the data lives**: local or cloud VM. Until Confidential VM ships, self-hosting is the higher-privacy ceiling.

## 5. The English/Chinese gap

As of 2026-10-08, systematic English-language coverage of Muse is dominated by press articles, and **systematic Chinese coverage is close to nonexistent** — mostly short-form video walkthroughs and scattered posts. What is missing in both languages is the combination this repository attempts: *official facts + the controversy + an ecosystem list + reproducible sources*.

---

**Inclusion criteria:** directly relevant to Muse / personal agents, and reachable. Referral / affiliate promotion repos are excluded even when they match the keyword. Found a dead link or a better resource? Open an issue.
